# Parity matrix — Argo CD v3.5.5 → Patrol CD

Generated by `go run ./hack/parity` from `docs/parity/areas/*.md` (feature inventory) and
`docs/parity/status.yaml` (Patrol implementation status). Do not edit by hand.

Status values: `not-started`, `in-progress`, `implemented` (code present, evidence incomplete),
`verified` (tests passed against real components), `verified-simulated` (tests passed against fakes/contract
servers only), `blocked`, `different-by-design` (intentional difference, documented).
Exposure flags: C=CRD, A=API, L=CLI, U=UI.

| [APP-085](areas/01-application.md#app-085-resource-change-driven-refresh) | Resource-change-driven refresh | GA | ---- | implemented | internal/controller/app/liveevents.go<br>internal/controller/app/reconciler.go | internal/controller/app/liveevents_test.go:TestOnResourceUpdated<br>internal/controller/app/liveevents_test.go:TestRefreshOrphanMonitors<br>internal/controller/app/reconciler_test.go:TestIndexKeys<br>internal/controller/integration/autosync_test.go:TestSelfHeal<br>internal/controller/integration/reconcile_test.go:TestRefreshAnnotationAndRecentRefresh<br>internal/controller/integration/orphans_test.go:TestOrphanedResourcesRefresh<br>_Managed (root) resources → recent comparison; descendants → tree-only refresh. Suppression by ignoreResourceUpdates runs before attribution; the Applications of the old and the new object are both refreshed; the changed Application object itself (same UID, as upstream) is skipped. A namespaced object attributed to no Application gives the orphan-monitoring Applications of its namespace and cluster a tree-only refresh (upstream: CompareWithRecent; the orphaned nodes, condition and gauge are the same, but a managed resource that was Missing at the last comparison stays Missing until the next one), found through in-memory field indexes (Applications by destination namespace, AppProjects with spec.orphanedResources); Applications outside the allowed namespaces or of other shards are skipped. Upstream matches orphan monitors by namespace on any cluster; Patrol also matches the event's cluster._ |

| [SRC-078](areas/02-sources-rendering.md#src-078-cmp-build-environment-argocd_app_-kube_version-kube_api_versions) | CMP build environment (`ARGOCD_APP_*`, `KUBE_VERSION`, `KUBE_API_VERSIONS`) | GA | ---- | implemented | internal/cmp/exec.go<br>internal/cmp/server.go<br>internal/cmp/options.go<br>internal/render/cmp.go<br>internal/render/env.go | internal/cmp/server_test.go:TestGenerateEndToEnd<br>internal/cmp/server_test.go:TestEnvironmentIsolation<br>internal/cmp/server_test.go:TestEnvironmentIsolationProcessEnv<br>internal/cmp/server_test.go:TestEnvironmentPassAll<br>internal/cmp/options_test.go:TestRunEndToEnd<br>internal/render/cmp_test.go:TestPluginNamedWithoutDiscovery<br>internal/render/cmp_test.go:TestPluginAutoDiscovery<br>internal/render/cmp_test.go:TestPluginParametersAndList<br>_Sidecar side. Different by design (ADR-0007 allow-list env): by default plugin commands get PATH/HOME/TMPDIR/LANG, not the sidecar's whole process environment as upstream (cmpserver/plugin/plugin.go append(os.Environ(), ...)), so plugins reading sidecar variables (argocd-vault-plugin AVP_*/VAULT_ADDR, proxies) need PATROL_CMP_PASS_ENV; `*` restores upstream (TestEnvironmentPassAll); the active mode is logged at startup. As upstream in every mode: the request env (build vars, KUBE_VERSION, KUBE_API_VERSIONS, ARGOCD_ENV_*, PARAM_*) follows the plugin env and wins on duplicates. See docs/architecture/components/cmp.md#migrating-plugins-from-argo-cd. The bearer header itself is sent by internal/git._ |

Additions: 404 for unknown provider paths, 429 per-source rate limit, 503 "Webhook server is busy" when the global in-flight body budget is exhausted, byte-bounded queue, untrusted values clipped in logs. Not yet mounted by patrol-server._ |
| [REPO-115](areas/03-repositories-clusters-webhooks.md#repo-115-webhookmaxpayloadsizemb) | `webhook.maxPayloadSizeMB` | GA | ---- | in-progress | internal/settings/parse_cm.go<br>internal/settings/settings.go<br>internal/webhook/handler.go<br>internal/webhook/config.go | internal/settings/parse_cm_test.go:TestParseEveryConfigMapKeyFamily<br>internal/settings/parse_cm_test.go:TestParseInvalidValuesAreReportedWithKeyNames<br>internal/settings/parse_cm_test.go:TestParseDefaults<br>internal/webhook/handler_test.go:TestProviderDeliveries<br>internal/webhook/handler_test.go:TestHTTPSemantics<br>internal/webhook/hardening_test.go:TestEffectivePayloadLimit<br>_Settings part only: parsing and validation of webhook.maxPayloadSizeMB (default 50; Webhook.MaxPayloadSize in bytes).; Raw body read with the webhook.maxPayloadSizeMB cap (Content-Length checked first, then limit+1 bytes read) before parsing; "0 MB" for limits below 1 MB as upstream; read per request, so changes apply without a restart. Difference: the setting is clamped to 25 MiB, the cap every pkg/scm verifier enforces (GitHub's own delivery limit), so the default (50) and larger values behave as 25 MiB and the 400 body says "under 25 MB" (upstream "under 50 MB"); values up to 25 behave as upstream; GHCR uses the same limit. See docs/architecture/components/webhook.md#gaps-and-intentional-differences (item 13)._ |
| [REPO-116](areas/03-repositories-clusters-webhooks.md#repo-116-webhook-secrets-in-argocd-secret) | Webhook secrets in `argocd-secret` | GA | ---- | in-progress | internal/settings/secrets.go<br>internal/webhook/secrets.go<br>internal/webhook/providers.go<br>internal/webhook/handler.go | internal/settings/secrets_test.go:TestWebhookSecretReferences<br>internal/settings/secrets_test.go:TestSecretValuesNeverLeak<br>internal/settings/retain_test.go:TestMissingSecretKeepsLastLoadedSecrets<br>internal/settings/manager_test.go:TestManagerKeepsLastLoadedSettingsWhenObjectsDisappear<br>internal/webhook/handler_test.go:TestSCMProviderSecrets<br>internal/webhook/handler_test.go:TestProviderDeliveries<br>internal/webhook/envtest_test.go:TestEnvtestWebhookRefresh<br>internal/webhook/hardening_test.go:TestUnsignedPolicy<br>_Settings part only: the five webhook secrets with $<key>/$<secret>:<key> resolution as Sensitive values; Manager subscribers see changes. A patrol-secret deleted after loading keeps its last secrets (upstream keeps its cached settings); Webhook.SecretsLoaded is false while it was never loaded, so handlers must reject payloads then. Verification of payloads is internal/webhook's.; patrol-secret webhook keys plus SCMProvider webhook.secretRef secrets (several candidates for rotation, fail closed when unreadable, tenant namespaces untrusted by default). Settings are read per request, so rotation (Azure DevOps credentials included) needs no restart. While patrol-secret was never loaded deliveries are answered 503 unless an SCMProvider secret verifies them. Unsigned deliveries are accepted when no webhook secret is configured anywhere (upstream). Intentional difference: once any webhook secret is configured (any provider, patrol-secret or trusted SCMProvider), unsigned deliveries are rejected for every provider, because the provider route comes from request headers and the repository from the unsigned payload (a forged delivery through a provider without a secret would bypass another provider's secret); AllowUnsignedPerProvider restores upstream, RequireSignatures rejects all unsigned deliveries. See docs/architecture/components/webhook.md#secrets-repo-116-t9 and item 14 of the differences._ |

Difference: the Application refresh patch carries the resourceVersion read through Config.APIReader (optimistic lock), so a hard refresh/hydrate requested or consumed between read and patch yields a real 409 and is re-decided (verified against envtest); upstream RefreshApp patches unconditionally. List does not fill (upstream). Inline health is returned as stored, without a tree read. A tree read is bounded (5 s); when it fails the health stays nil, the request succeeds and no refresh is requested; a watch stream whose controller does not answer stops reading for 30 s. Nothing is written to the Application.; Health written to status.resources only with PersistResourceHealth; ResourceHealthSource inline vs appTree; tree nodes carry health. The API server's fill-in from the tree is in api-applications.yaml. Switching modes (converging on the next reconcile) has no dedicated test.; Settings part: patrol-cmd-params-cm key(s) controller.resource.health.persist with upstream env var (ARGOCD_ renamed PATROL_), flag name, default, bounds and fallback warnings. Consumed by the controller (internal/server/controller.go, compare.Deps.PersistResourceHealth; compare.yaml) and the API server's tree fill-in (api-applications.yaml)._ |

A transient error before the dry run completed reports no rows, so the dry run is never skipped._ |

| [ASET-108](areas/06-applicationset.md#aset-108-templated-project-field-and-applicationset-trust-model) | Templated `project` field and ApplicationSet trust model | GA | CA-- | implemented | internal/apiserver/services/applicationsets/handlers.go<br>internal/appset/generate.go<br>internal/appset/reconciler.go<br>internal/appset/template/render.go<br>internal/appset/generators/git.go | internal/apiserver/services/applicationsets/applicationsets_test.go:TestCreateValidation<br>internal/apiserver/services/applicationsets/applicationsets_test.go:TestCreateAuthorization<br>internal/apiserver/services/applicationsets/limits_projects_test.go:TestActionsOnExistingNeedEveryProject<br>internal/apiserver/services/applicationsets/generator_internal_test.go:TestRBACObjects<br>internal/appset/generate_test.go:TestValidateForAPI<br>internal/appset/generate_test.go:TestProjectsCoverGeneratorTemplates<br>internal/appset/generate_test.go:TestGenerateApplicationsTemplatePatch<br>internal/appset/reconciler_test.go:TestReconcileTemplatedProjectEscalation<br>internal/appset/envtest_test.go:TestEnvtestTemplatedProjectEscalation<br>internal/appset/envtest_test.go:TestEnvtestGitCredentialScope<br>_API side: templated projects (spec.template and generator-level templates) are rejected for create, dry-run and generate; create is authorized on every project of internal/appset Projects, so a generator template cannot escalate into another project. | [TEN-092](areas/07-projects-rbac-auth.md#ten-092-initial-admin-password) | Initial admin password | GA | --L- | verified | internal/server/bootstrap_secrets.go<br>internal/server/bootstrap_cmd.go | internal/server/bootstrap_secrets_test.go:TestBootstrapFreshInstall<br>internal/server/bootstrap_secrets_test.go:TestBootstrapAdminDisabled<br>internal/server/bootstrap_secrets_test.go:TestBootstrapKeepsOperatorMaterial<br>internal/server/bootstrap_secrets_test.go:TestBootstrapCrashRecovery<br>internal/server/bootstrap_secrets_test.go:TestGenerateInitialAdminPassword<br>internal/server/bootstrap_stale_test.go:TestBootstrapStaleInitialSecret<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:bootstrap (fresh install on kind; the init container converged and created patrol-initial-admin-secret)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:api (admin login with the generated password returns 200)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh --lifecycle (a re-install generates a new password; the old initial and the changed password get 401)<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-19-kind-validation.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-18-bootstrap-dv6.md<br>_Generated when admin is enabled and has no hash (disabled admin, including a malformed admin.enabled value, generates nothing); an existing hash is never regenerated and a re-run writes nothing (resourceVersions unchanged); login verified through settings.Parse and auth.Authenticator. Runs as the separate `patrolcd bootstrap` step (init container), not inside patrol-server. A re-install that re-creates patrol-secret gets a new password (the stale initial Secret is replaced; neither the old initial nor the previously changed password logs in). Fresh-install generation, login and the re-install replacement are asserted on kind (2026-10-19, chart default profile, kubelet-run init container, the real server's login API); "a restart does not rotate" is asserted by the second bootstrap run of TestBootstrapFreshInstall against envtest (no write, same password logs in), not by a pod restart on kind. Intentional differences (bootstrap.md): 24-character [A-Za-z0-9] password, an initial Secret not older than patrol-secret reused instead of regenerated, mtime of an existing hash not initialized. Limits: one replica, kindnet, linux/arm64._ |

The stream polls with ETags (3 s) and on Application events, authorizes every event, ends with DELETED or a 403 error event. Server-side diff is POST with a JSON body (contract) instead of GET with repeated query parameters; Secret results are masked again at the API. The API checks live states against their declared identities (upstream messages) and refuses a target manifest paired with a live state that names another object (400; upstream does not check targets, so the controller would dry-run apply any object with its credentials).; Internal controller API and routing client only; the public /api/v1 routes, RBAC and the watch stream are internal/apiserver (the tree endpoint offers ETag/If-None-Match for polling)._ |

| [API-049](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-049-proxy-extensions--forwarding-backend-selection-outgoing-headers-and-metrics) | Proxy extensions — forwarding, backend selection, outgoing headers and metrics | Beta | -A-- | implemented | internal/apiserver/services/extensions/proxy.go<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/registry.go<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/metrics.go<br>pkg/apiclient/extensions.go | internal/apiserver/services/extensions/extensions_test.go:TestProxyForwardsWithHeaderContract<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/extensions_test.go:TestProxyGoClientAndSSOIdentity<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/extensions_test.go:TestProxyRoutesByDestination<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/extensions_test.go:TestProxyLimitsAndFailures<br>internal/apiserver/services/extensions/extensions_test.go:TestProxyWebSocket<br>pkg/apiclient/appsetsmisc_client_test.go:TestInvokeExtensionClient<br>_Outgoing headers Patrol-Namespace, Patrol-Target-Cluster-Name/URL, Patrol-User-Id, Patrol-Username, Patrol-User-Groups. Stricter than upstream: only allow-listed request headers are forwarded (upstream forwards all but Authorization and Cookie), Set-Cookie is removed from responses, bodies are capped, the whole exchange has a timeout, WebSocket upgrades are off by default; a destination is also matched through its resolved cluster's name or server. Metrics patrol_server_extension_requests_total and patrol_server_extension_request_duration_seconds._ |

| [API-051](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-051-cli-global-flags-argocd_opts-and-environment-variables) | CLI global flags, `ARGOCD_OPTS` and environment variables | GA | --L- | verified | internal/cli/cmdutil/flags.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/opts.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/client.go<br>internal/cli/root.go | internal/cli/session_test.go:TestServerAndTokenSources<br>internal/cli/root_test.go:TestInvalidInvocations<br>internal/cli/root_test.go:TestHelpGolden<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/cmdutil_test.go:TestParseOpts<br>_PATROL_OPTS (shell-quoted, --flag=value form, "PATROL_OPTS invalid at '<x>'"), PATROL_SERVER, PATROL_AUTH_TOKEN, PATROL_SERVER_NAME, PATROL_APPLICATION_CONTROLLER_NAME, PATROL_REPO_SERVER_NAME; precedence flag > PATROL_OPTS > environment > configuration. ARGOCD_OPTS/ARGOCD_SERVER/ARGOCD_AUTH_TOKEN are warned migration aliases (ARGOCD_OPTS --auth-token dropped; ARGOCD_AUTH_TOKEN only sent to ARGOCD_SERVER). --argocd-context is a hidden alias of --patrol-context; --redis-* accepted and deprecated (ADR-0004)._ |
| [API-052](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-052-cli-local-configuration-file-and-contexts) | CLI local configuration file and contexts | GA | --L- | verified | internal/cli/config/config.go<br>internal/cli/config/perm_unix.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/client.go | internal/cli/session_test.go:TestLoginContextsLogout<br>internal/cli/session_test.go:TestServerAndTokenSources<br>internal/cli/session_test.go:TestSSOLoginAndRefresh<br>internal/cli/config/config_test.go:TestWriteReadRoundTrip<br>internal/cli/config/config_test.go:TestReadErrors<br>internal/cli/config/config_test.go:TestContextOperations<br>internal/cli/config/config_test.go:TestDefaultPath<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/target_test.go:TestResolveTargetScopesContextCredentials<br>_~/.config/patrol/config (PATROL_CONFIG, PATROL_CONFIG_DIR, XDG_CONFIG_HOME), upstream YAML keys, atomic 0600 writes, 0600/0400 enforced on read. Context credentials and TLS data apply only to the context's own server (hardening): a requested --port-forward / --port-forward-namespace or --core is its own effective server and reuses only a port-forward / core context (no token, refresh token, SSO refresh or renewed-token storage for a remote context). The expired-SSO refresh path is exercised against a fake OIDC provider. ARGOCD_CONFIG_DIR and ~/.argocd are not read._ |
| [API-053](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-053-cli-transport-selection-native-grpc-grpc-web-fallback-tls-headers-retries) | CLI transport selection: native gRPC, gRPC-Web fallback, TLS, headers, retries | GA | --L- | verified | internal/cli/cmdutil/client.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/debuglog.go<br>internal/cli/transport/transport.go | internal/cli/transport_test.go:TestRootPath<br>internal/cli/transport_test.go:TestHTTPRetriesAndHeaders<br>internal/cli/session_test.go:TestLoginTLS<br>internal/cli/root_test.go:TestInvalidInvocations<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/cmdutil_test.go:TestDebugTransportRedacts<br>_Transport is JSON over HTTP (ADR-0005): no gRPC probe or gRPC-Web fallback; --grpc-web is accepted and ignored, --grpc-web-root-path is the server root path. TLS by default, --plaintext, --insecure, --server-crt, client certificates, -H headers, User-Agent patrol-cli/<version>, --http-retry-max (502/503/504 and transport errors on GET)._ |
| [API-054](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-054-cli-port-forward-mode) | CLI port-forward mode | GA | --L- | in-progress | internal/cli/transport/podstream.go<br>internal/cli/transport/portforward.go<br>internal/cli/core/pods.go<br>internal/cli/transport/portforward.go | internal/cli/core/pods_internal_test.go:TestPortForwardRouting<br>internal/cli/core/pods_internal_test.go:TestPodRouterRepoServer<br>internal/cli/core/pods_internal_test.go:TestPodRouterController<br>internal/cli/transport/podstream_test.go:TestPodDialerArgumentsAndClose<br>internal/cli/portforward_test.go:TestPortForward<br>internal/cli/portforward_test.go:TestPortForwardDoesNotReuseRemoteContext<br>internal/cli/transport/transport_test.go:TestSelectReadyPod<br>_Core mode reaches the repo-server and the controller tree API through the port-forward machinery of --port-forward without a local listener (PodDialer: one v4.channel.k8s.io WebSocket stream per connection, in-memory net.Conn). Pod selection, controller pod verification (label or StatefulSet owner, Running and Ready), caching, reselection after a failed dial and 403 handling are tested with a fake clientset and a fake Kubernetes API speaking the WebSocket protocol. A manual run on kind (2026-10-19, run record step 15) reached the live repo-server through a kubelet-backed port-forward (`app manifests` rendered by it), and `app list`, `app get`, `app diff` and `app history` worked against the live cluster; no committed script or test repeats it, so the entry stays in-progress.; Pod selection and the v4.channel.k8s.io WebSocket protocol run against a fake Kubernetes API (envtest has no kubelet) bridging to the real patrol API server over TLS (insecure forced). WebSocket instead of SPDY (client-go port-forward packages are not in the module graph)._ |
| [API-055](areas/08-api-cli-ui-extensions.md#api-055-cli-core-headless-mode) | CLI core (headless) mode | GA | --L- | implemented | internal/cli/core/core.go<br>internal/cli/core/memtransport.go<br>internal/cli/core/register.go<br>internal/cli/core/kubecontexts.go<br>internal/cli/core/endpoints.go<br>internal/server/core.go<br>internal/server/core_support.go<br>internal/server/core_clusters.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/flags.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/client.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/kube.go<br>cmd/patrol/main.go<br>internal/cli/transport/transport.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/client.go<br>internal/cli/cmdutil/kube.go | internal/cli/core/e2e_test.go:TestCoreAppLifecycle<br>internal/cli/core/e2e_test.go:TestCoreKubernetesRBACIsTheAuthorization<br>internal/cli/core/kubeflags_e2e_test.go:TestCoreNamespaceFlag<br>internal/cli/core/kubeflags_e2e_test.go:TestCoreClusterCommandsUseCallerCredentials<br>internal/cli/core/register_test.go:TestKubectlFlagsOnSubcommands<br>internal/cli/core/shutdown_test.go:TestCoreConnectionInProcessAndCleanShutdown<br>internal/cli/core/register_test.go:TestConflictingFlags<br>internal/cli/core/register_test.go:TestUnsupportedCommands<br>internal/cli/core/memtransport_test.go:TestHandlerTransportStreams<br>internal/cli/core/memtransport_test.go:TestHandlerTransportWebSocket<br>internal/cli/core/endpoints_internal_test.go:TestNamespaceAndDestinations<br>internal/cli/core/endpoints_internal_test.go:TestNoContextErrorIsUserFacing<br>internal/apiserver/services/applications/clusterconfig_error_test.go:TestClusterConfigUnavailable<br>internal/server/core_test.go:TestCoreHandlerEnvtest<br>internal/server/core_test.go:TestCoreHandlerDegrades<br>internal/server/core_test.go:TestCoreRoutesMatchTheServer<br>internal/server/core_clusters_test.go:TestCoreClusterConnectionsUseCallerCredentials<br>internal/cli/root_test.go:TestInvalidInvocations<br>_--core, PATROL_CORE, PATROL_OPTS --core and a stored core context run the patrol-server API in the CLI process (no listener; in-memory RoundTripper) with authentication and Patrol RBAC disabled; every Kubernetes call uses the kubeconfig credentials (audit log checked: only the caller, no impersonation), destination clusters included: cluster connection checks and rotate-auth use the caller's kubeconfig context for the server, never the stored Cluster Secret credentials (without a context: no check, rotate-auth refused; upstream uses the stored credentials). Namespace from -n/--namespace (persistent kubectl flags, accepted by every subcommand; upstream only on the group command), the kube context, else "patrol" (upstream: "default"). Operation spec.initiatedBy is the SelfSubjectReview user (admitted by the admission policy without the impersonate verb). Session commands (relogin, account update-password/generate-token/delete-token/session-token) and admin dashboard --core are refused with explicit messages (dashboard: different by design, no unauthenticated in-process UI). --core with --server/--port-forward and core mode with --auth-token are refused. No Redis (ADR-0004). Live repo-server/controller access depends on API-054. A destination cluster without a matching kubeconfig context (e.g. `app logs` for the kind Cluster https://patrol-target-control-plane:6443, a container-network name the host's kubeconfig does not have) answers with the explanation instead of a bare 503 (finding KV-7: noContextError opts in through UserMessage; other provider errors stay hidden). Core mode ran against kind only by hand (2026-10-19, run record step 15), not in a committed test.; --core, login --core and the kubectl client flags are plumbed through transport.Provider; the provider (internal/cli/core, registered by cmd/patrol) and its evidence are recorded in docs/parity/status/cli-core-mode.yaml. Builds without the registrar still fail with "core mode not available in this build" (tested here)._ |

"New application" opens the creation wizard (?new=1) from src/pages/create._ |

wildcard patterns from a shared link in linear-ish time<br>_Shell side only: the details page mounts the resource views (owned by apps-resources) through ResourceViewsSlot, forwarding app, onSyncResources (selective sync from the resource drawer), onOpenApplication (tree root) and fullscreenLogs; tree data is the shared useResourceTree cache entry and selection the `node` URL parameter. A built-in resource table is the fallback when the module is absent.; Tree (layered left-to-right SVG graph with Patrol's own layout: longest-path layering, tidy rows, bus edge routing; viewport culling, level of detail, zoom/pan by pointer, wheel and keyboard, fit to screen, zoom persisted), Pods (by node with host resources and propagated node labels, by parent, by top-level resource; `podSortMode` param and `pref.cd.patrol.example/default-pod-sort`), Network and List views; `view` param and `pref.cd.patrol.example/default-view`. Filters in URL params (`name` with wildcards, `kind`, `sync` where OutOfSync includes Unknown and hooks ignore the sync filter, `health`, `namespace`, `orphaned` offered when the project monitors orphaned resources, `hooks`). Group similar nodes, "show N more" per parent, collapse/expand, and an ARIA tree outline. The tree is live through the resource-tree SSE stream. App View extensions are API-123. The health filter applies to each node (not to the application root). The `name` wildcard filter is a two-pointer glob matcher (O(name x pattern)), not a RegExp, so a crafted link cannot stall the page._ |

Parameters/Sources (Helm, Kustomize, Directory, Plugin editors, add/remove sources with refs) and Manifest (YAML of metadata and spec, validated) tabs, plus Application resource-tab extensions. Saves PUT the whole object: the user's changes (draft vs. the version it is based on) are applied onto the latest watched version, so status-only updates do not conflict; when labels, annotations, finalizers or the spec changed on the server, saving is held and the changed paths are listed until the user rebases (3-way merge: untouched fields take server values, both-changed fields keep the user's value and are listed as conflicts), and a 409 offers the same rebase. The summary mounts the notification subscriptions editor (NOTIF-109, src/pages/notifications). The manifest is saved with PUT, not UpdateSpec. Missing: the app EVENTS tab; the diff is a separate drawer._ |

| ID | Feature | Maturity | Exp | Status | Implementation | Tests / evidence |

| [NOTIF-001](areas/09-notifications.md#notif-001-notifications-controller-process-lifecycle) | Notifications controller process lifecycle | GA | CALU | in-progress | internal/notifications/controller/run.go | internal/notifications/controller/envtest_test.go:TestControllerDeliversOnTransitionsAndDeduplicatesAcrossRestart<br>internal/notifications/controller/envtest_config_test.go:TestConcurrentProcessorsWithLeaderElection<br>internal/notifications/controller/envtest_config_test.go:TestRunRejectsInvalidConfiguration<br>_Run(ctx, Config) starts informers for Applications, AppProjects and the notifications ConfigMap/Secret, waits for sync, returns nil on cancellation after recording deliveries. Different by design: regular expressions are Go RE2, upstream uses regexp2, so lookaround entries (e.g. the documented /^((?!not-allowed).)*$/) match upstream but never here, as in internal/webhook and internal/project (notifications-controller.md#deviations-deliberate). Flag/env wiring is the command's.; Settings part only: patrol-cmd-params-cm key(s) application.namespaces with upstream env var (ARGOCD_ renamed PATROL_), flag name, default, bounds and fallback warnings; the consuming component applies the semantics._ |

| [OPS-001](areas/10-operations.md#ops-001-cluster-scoped-standard-install-bundle-manifestsinstallyaml) | Cluster-scoped standard install bundle (`manifests/install.yaml`) | GA | -ALU | in-progress | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/values.yaml<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd<br>deploy/manifests/install.yaml<br>deploy/manifests/install.yaml<br>deploy/manifests/cluster-install<br>deploy/manifests/profiles/install.yaml<br>hack/genmanifests | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/default.yaml (make helm-lint, make helm-template)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:pods (exactly 5 Patrol pods, all Running and ready, 0 restarts)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:security (per component uid 999, an empty capability bounding set, no_new_privs, root file system mounted read-only; until the review it checked the effective capabilities and a failing write instead)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:bootstrap and api (init container converged; /healthz 200 through NodePort 30443; anonymous project list 401; admin login 200; AppProject default exists)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:sync (7 fixture Applications of seven source types synced through the API, ending Synced/Healthy/Succeeded; their workloads fully available on patrol-target)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:drift (a Deployment scaled on patrol-target reported OutOfSync; a new sync restores 1 replica)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh --lifecycle (helm rollback to revision 1, upgrade, uninstall keeps the 8 CRDs and 7 Applications, install again, workloads on patrol-target keep running)<br>test/deploy/apivalidation_test.go:TestAPIServerDryRun<br>test/deploy/helmlifecycle_test.go:TestHelmLifecycle<br>test/deploy/manifests_test.go:TestStaticManifestsApply<br>test/deploy/manifests_test.go:TestStaticManifestsMatchChart<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACSufficiency<br>hack/genmanifests/main_test.go:TestManifestsAreCurrent<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-19-kind-validation.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-chart-static.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-manifests-static.md<br>_The default chart values render the cluster-scoped install: 8 CRDs plus the Operation ValidatingAdmissionPolicy and binding, the settings ConfigMaps (patrol-cm with the shipped defaults), an empty patrol-secret, patrol-notifications-cm/-secret, server Deployment, controller StatefulSet, repo-server, ApplicationSet and notifications controllers, Services, ServiceAccounts, Roles and ClusterRoles, NetworkPolicies, PDBs. No Redis (ADR-0004); Dex off by default. Cluster-scoped objects are named <namespace>-<prefix>-<component> and bind ServiceAccounts of the release namespace, so no ClusterRoleBinding patch is needed for another namespace (upstream edge case). Installed on kind (2026-10-19) into namespace patrol of patrol-control with hack/kind/values.yaml, which differs from the defaults only in the image (patrol-cd/patrol-cd:dev, loaded into the nodes), the server Service type (NodePort 30443) and the repo-server egress ports (22, 80, 443, 2222, 5000, 8080; finding KV-3): the checks listed in tests passed in a clean-room run (make kind-down image kind-up kind-fixtures kind-install, then make kind-verify VERIFY_FLAGS=--lifecycle; V-0031, with the script as it was before the review; the closing run with the current script is V-0032). The only destination was the registered external cluster patrol-target; an Application targeting https://kubernetes.default.svc ran only in the envtest harness (deploy-validation.yaml). helm uninstall removes chart-managed ConfigMaps, so host keys added to patrol-ssh-known-hosts-cm after the install are lost on a re-install (KV-10). Limits: one CNI (kindnet), linux/arm64, one replica per component, Kubernetes v1.36.4; seccomp and the repo-server's missing service account token were observed by hand only. Status implemented, not verified (review of 2026-10-19, finding F7): the area requires, besides all pods Ready, an Application targeting https://kubernetes.default.svc that syncs, a golden inventory of the installed objects, and recovery (delete a component's pod: recreated and functional; re-apply is idempotent). On a cluster with nodes the in-cluster destination and the pod-deletion recovery were not exercised (the in-cluster sync and the idempotent no-op upgrade ran only against envtest with emulated pods, deploy-validation.yaml, and no harness deletes a pod), so the kind checks listed in tests cover part of the feature. hack/kind/install.sh (make kind-install) installs the chart for these checks but asserts nothing beyond helm's --wait.; Default profile: every rendered object passes a strict server-side dry run without warnings; helm install, no-op upgrade (none of the 60 rendered objects changed: resourceVersion compared, or generation, labels and annotations for the CRDs and the admission policy, whose status the API server writes; generated keys kept), upgrade with values, image tag, common labels and a disabled component (selectors and Service clusterIPs unchanged), rollback, uninstall (CRDs and admission policy kept) and re-install work against a kube-apiserver; install.yaml server-side applies, re-applies without changes and deletes. The rendered components run as their service accounts with exactly the rendered RBAC and sync an Application to the local cluster without a denied request. In this harness no pod ran on a node. On kind (2026-10-19) the chart's default profile ran with real pods and passed hack/kind/verify-install.sh (see chart.yaml OPS-001); install.yaml (the cluster-install overlay with the local image) was applied to patrol-target only by hand (61 objects, five pods Running, admin login 200 through a port-forward; the controllers waited about a minute for patrol-internal-token with FailedMount events until the server's bootstrap created it), which is not a test, so this entry stays in-progress.; install.yaml = kustomize build deploy/manifests/cluster-install = the chart defaults rendered for release/namespace patrol without Helm metadata, plus the namespace patrol (Pod Security restricted labels): 61 objects (namespace, 8 CRDs, admission policy and binding, 7 ConfigMaps, 2 empty Secrets, 5 ServiceAccounts, 5 Roles/RoleBindings, 2 ClusterRoles/ClusterRoleBindings, 7 Services, 4 Deployments, the controller StatefulSet, 5 NetworkPolicies, 5 PDBs). Objects carry namespace patrol (a wrong -n fails) and the ClusterRoleBindings bind patrol ServiceAccounts; a kustomize namespace overlay renames the namespace and rewrites the subjects (checked against the API server). Server-side applied to a kube-apiserver and re-applied without changes. On kind (2026-10-19) the documented image overlay of deploy/manifests/cluster-install was built with kustomize and applied with kubectl apply --server-side --force-conflicts to patrol-target by hand: 61 objects, five pods Running without restarts, bootstrap converged, admin login 200 through a port-forward; the controllers' pods waited about a minute for patrol-internal-token (FailedMount events) until the server pod's bootstrap created it; kubectl delete -f removed it again. That run is not a committed test, so the entry stays in-progress._ |

Installed only against envtest (deploy-validation.yaml); not on a cluster with nodes (the kind validation of 2026-10-19 installed the default profile only).; Namespaced profile installed next to a cluster-wide release (CRDs from the latter); with the local cluster registered with explicit namespaces the controller syncs into the release namespace with Roles only; SubjectAccessReviews confirm no access outside the release namespace and rbac.managedNamespaces and no cluster-scoped reads. rbac.managedNamespaces must exist before the install (finding DV-3).; rbac.scope=namespaced without CRDs and admission policy: 50 objects, no namespace, CRD, admission policy, ClusterRole or ClusterRoleBinding (the generator refuses any cluster-scoped object or object outside namespace patrol in namespace-scoped variants). Server dry run against a kube-apiserver with crds.yaml installed passed; not run with pods. Applications on the local cluster need a Cluster object for https://kubernetes.default.svc listing the namespace (the synthetic in-cluster cluster has no namespace list, so the live-state cache would list cluster-wide); documented in docs/guides/manifests.md. Only the chart's equivalent profile with that object runs in the deployment harness (TestRBACSufficiency/namespaced), not this file._ |

Run only in the envtest harness with emulated pods (deploy-validation.yaml); not installed on a cluster with nodes.; Hydrator profile: the commit-server starts from its rendered arguments without a service account token and answers its probes; the controller runs with hydrator.enabled. No hydration was exercised (no write repository in the scenario).; All five *-with-hydrator files exist and contain the commit-server (Deployment, Service, ServiceAccount, NetworkPolicy, PDB) and hydrator.enabled "true" plus commit.server in patrol-cmd-params-cm, the HA ones included (upstream's HA quirk, commit-server without hydrator.enabled, is not reproduced; documented). Switching back to a plain file removes the two keys but does not delete the commit-server objects (kubectl does not prune; cleanup command documented and executed against the API server). Not run with pods.; Settings part only: patrol-cmd-params-cm key(s) hydrator.enabled, commit.server with upstream env var (ARGOCD_ renamed PATROL_), flag name, default, bounds and fallback warnings; the consuming component applies the semantics._ |
| [OPS-006](areas/10-operations.md#ops-006-crd-bundle-and-server-side-apply-installation-requirement) | CRD bundle and server-side-apply installation requirement | GA | ---- | in-progress | hack/helmdocs/crds.go<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/crds<br>deploy/manifests/crds.yaml<br>deploy/manifests/crds | hack/helmdocs/main_test.go:TestChartIsCurrent<br>hack/helmdocs/main_test.go:TestTemplateCRDRejectsUnexpectedLayout<br>hack/genmanifests/main_test.go:TestManifestsAreCurrent<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-chart-static.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-manifests-static.md<br>_The CRDs are templates generated from deploy/crds (byte-identical bodies, checked by `make helm-verify`), so `helm upgrade` upgrades them, with helm.sh/resource-policy: keep. Server-side apply instructions for non-Helm installs in the guide. Installed by helm against envtest (test/deploy TestHelmLifecycle) and on kind (2026-10-19): hack/kind/verify-install.sh --lifecycle asserts that the 8 CRDs survive helm uninstall and that helm install succeeds again over them. Not exercised: an upgrade that changes a CRD (every upgrade on kind used the same chart) and the self-managed upgrade with ServerSideApply=true, so the row stays in-progress.; crds.yaml (and the crds/ base) hold the 8 CRDs, the Operation ValidatingAdmissionPolicy and its binding with the chart's labels and without helm.sh/resource-policy. Against a kube-apiserver 1.36: client-side apply fails with 'The CustomResourceDefinition "applicationsets.cd.patrol.example" is invalid: metadata.annotations: Too long: may not be more than 262144 bytes'; kubectl apply --server-side --force-conflicts creates all ten objects and every CRD becomes Established. Every file header and the guide use server-side apply. Self-management with ServerSideApply=true is not exercised here._ |
| [OPS-007](areas/10-operations.md#ops-007-cluster-scope-rbac-bundle-manifestscluster-rbac) | Cluster-scope RBAC bundle (`manifests/cluster-rbac`) | GA | ---- | in-progress | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/rbac<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/rbac | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/default.yaml<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/hardened.yaml<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACSufficiency<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACMissingRuleDetected<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-chart-static.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md<br>_Controller ClusterRole cluster-admin-equivalent by default (rbac.controller.clusterAdmin) or a rules list (ci/hardened.yaml names the writable resources, so no subresource, node, namespace or RBAC writes, but its read-everything rule and workload/Secret writes still allow escalation; documented in installation.md "Hardened installation"); server ClusterRole get/patch/delete any resource, list events, get pods and pods/log, create Jobs, optional pods/exec and serviceaccounts impersonate. The ApplicationSet controller has no ClusterRole unless applicationSetNamespaces needs one (upstream grants it unconditionally). Rules derived from the components' API calls; their sufficiency was exercised against envtest with the real binaries and the RBAC authorizer (deploy-validation.yaml OPS-007). On kind (2026-10-19) the default ClusterRoles were in effect while the components worked, but no check there asserts a cluster-scoped rule, and every Application targeted the external cluster patrol-target (reached with the registered Cluster's ServiceAccount token, not these ClusterRoles), so the row stays in-progress.; Cluster-scoped rules exercised by the real binaries (default, core, hardened, minimal, hydrator, ingress-nginx, apps-in-any-namespace runs): no request denied in the audit log, no "forbidden" in component logs. SubjectAccessReviews: controller cluster-admin by default (documented), limited by rbac.controller.rules in the hardened profile (an Application still syncs; no RBAC, admission, node, namespace or subresource writes such as serviceaccounts/token or pods/exec, while Secret writes in any namespace and get on nodes/proxy remain, as documented), patrol-server may read but not create on the local cluster, no access with rbac.server.localCluster=false. Unexercised verbs are listed in the findings (informational)._ |
| [OPS-008](areas/10-operations.md#ops-008-namespaced-component-roles-rolebindings-and-serviceaccounts) | Namespaced component Roles, RoleBindings and ServiceAccounts | GA | ---- | in-progress | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/rbac/_rules.tpl<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/rbac | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/all-features.yaml (make helm-lint)<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACSufficiency<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACMissingRuleDetected<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-chart-static.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md<br>_Per-component Roles in the release namespace and in literal application/ApplicationSet namespaces; repo-server, commit-server and Dex have ServiceAccounts without RBAC and no mounted token; the notifications controller lists and watches every ConfigMap and Secret of the release namespace (settings informers, repository lister; as upstream) and gets its named ConfigMaps and Secret; a separate bootstrap Role (create Secrets; get the three bootstrap Secrets; update patrol-secret; delete patrol-initial-admin-secret only; get patrol-cm) bound to the primary workload. With serviceAccount.create=false the render fails unless serviceAccount.name is set (never the namespace's default ServiceAccount; hack/helmdocs TestServiceAccountNameRequired, ci/invalid/serviceaccount-without-name.yaml). Operations creators get the impersonate verb the admission policy checks. Sufficiency exercised against envtest (deploy-validation.yaml OPS-008, audit log). On kind (2026-10-19, default profile) the components ran with these Roles while hack/kind/verify-install.sh passed (bootstrap, login, default AppProject, Operations created by API syncs, a re-install replacing the stale initial admin Secret with the bootstrap Role's delete rule), but no kind check asserts a rule or the absence of forbidden requests; the repo-server running without a token was observed by hand only. The row stays in-progress.; Namespaced Roles bound to the components' service accounts are sufficient for bootstrap, patrol-server (login, repositories, Applications, ApplicationSets, manual sync), the controller (shard Lease, sync, Operations), the ApplicationSet controller (leader election, list generator) and the notifications controller (leader election); the repo-server and commit-server run without a token. Removing the notifications Lease rules or patrol-server's AppProject rule is detected (audit 403 and "forbidden" log lines)._ |
| [OPS-009](areas/10-operations.md#ops-009-component-services-and-port-map) | Component Services and port map | GA | ---- | in-progress | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates<br>internal/server/commands.go<br>internal/server/server.go<br>internal/server/controller.go<br>internal/server/appset.go<br>internal/server/notifications.go<br>internal/server/components.go | test/deploy/wiring_test.go:TestWiring<br>test/deploy/rbac_test.go:TestRBACSufficiency<br>cmd/patrolcd/main_test.go:TestHelpGolden<br>internal/server/smoke_test.go:TestServerRunSmoke<br>internal/server/smoke_test.go:TestControllerRunSmoke<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md<br>_Every listener computed from the parsed arguments (wiring.md port table) is a declared containerPort and vice versa; every Service selects one pod template and targets declared ports; the headless controller Service carries 8090. The harness reaches every component through its Service mapping and its readiness, liveness and startup probes answer 200 (emulated pods, no kube-proxy). On kind (2026-10-19, default profile) hack/kind/verify-install.sh reaches patrol-server's https port through NodePort 30443 and the metrics Services patrol-controller-metrics:8082, patrol-server-metrics:8083, patrol-repo-server:8084 and patrol-notifications-controller-metrics:9001 from another namespace; the repo-server's 8081 is used by the syncs but not checked on its own, and the ApplicationSet controller's Service, the HTTP-to-HTTPS redirect and the commit-server and Dex Services were not exercised on kind, so the entry stays in-progress.; Default listeners server 8080/8083, repo-server 8081/8084, controller 8082 (+ tree API 8090, ADR-0004/0008), appset 8080/8081 (webhook listener off by default), notifications 9001, commit-server 8086/8087; no Dex or Redis. Defaults are pinned by the help goldens; the smoke tests bind port 0. Services are the manifests owner's._ |
| [OPS-010](areas/10-operations.md#ops-010-networkpolicies) | NetworkPolicies | GA | ---- | in-progress | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/networkpolicy<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/templates/networkpolicy | deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/hardened.yaml (make helm-lint)<br>deploy/helm/patrol-cd/ci/ingress-nginx.yaml<br>hack/helmdocs/behavior_test.go:TestNetworkPolicyExcept<br>hack/helmdocs/render_test.go:TestRenderedProfiles<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:netpol (repo-server egress allowed on the configured ports, refused to kubernetes.default.svc:443 and controller 8090)<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:metrics (four metrics endpoints answer from another namespace)<br>test/deploy/apivalidation_test.go:TestAPIServerDryRun<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-19-kind-validation.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-chart-static.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md<br>_Stricter than upstream (ingress-only, server open): every component restricts ingress and egress (DNS, API server, peer components, configurable external CIDR/port sets, metrics scrapers). except entries are attached only to the CIDRs that strictly contain them (render check of every profile), and the repo-server and commit-server egress excludes the cloud metadata endpoints by default (networkPolicy.excludeMetadataEndpoints). Enforcement was exercised on kind (2026-10-19, kindnet enforces NetworkPolicy; default profile with hack/kind/values.yaml, which adds repo-server egress ports 2222, 5000 and 8080). hack/kind/verify-install.sh asserts from the repo-server pod: the git server's Services on 80 and 22 (pods on 8080 and 2222) and patrol-registry:5000 are reachable; kubernetes.default.svc:443 and the controller's port 8090 are not; and the four metrics endpoints (8082, 8083, 8084, 9001) answer from namespace patrol-e2e; everything else in the run (bootstrap, API, 7 syncs, drift) worked with the policies enforced. Observed (KV-3): NetworkPolicy ports are the destination pod's ports after Service translation, so the default repo-server egress ports (22, 80, 443) block in-cluster servers whose pods listen on other ports (the fixtures until the extra ports were set); kindnet matches pod IPs with an ipBlock of 0.0.0.0/0 (temporary manual policies, other CNIs may not). Both refusals are explained by the repo-server's egress ports alone (the kind API server endpoint listens on 6443; 8090 is not listed), so they show neither a rule that excludes those peers nor the controller's ingress policy at work; an API server endpoint on 443 would match the default repo-server egress (inferred from the policy, not tested; the repo-server has no token). Not tested: any ingress refusal (e.g. an unlisted pod reaching repo-server 8081 or controller 8090), the egress limits of the other components, the metadata-endpoint exclusion, other CNIs. The row stays in-progress.; NetworkPolicies of every profile pass strict server-side validation. This harness does not exercise enforcement (envtest has no CNI); the kind validation of 2026-10-19 exercised the default profile's policies on kindnet, partially (see chart.yaml OPS-010)._ |

A re-install's bootstrap keeps the internal token and replaces the initial admin Secret, which predates the re-rendered patrol-secret (finding DV-6, fixed): new password and Secret UID; the new password logs in through the server's login path (internal/auth), the stale initial password and the password changed before the uninstall do not. On kind (2026-10-19, default profile, server pod only) hack/kind/verify-install.sh asserts that the init container converged and created patrol-initial-admin-secret, and with --lifecycle the DV-6 behavior through the running server: after a password change through the API, helm uninstall and install, the Secret has a new UID, the changed and the old initial passwords get 401 and the new one 200. The internal token surviving the uninstall was observed by hand only, and the core profile (bootstrap on the controller pod, optional token mount) never ran on a kubelet, so the entry stays in-progress.; All patrol-secret keys are read; server.secretkey bootstrap with conflict tolerance is implemented. Initial admin password generation lives in `patrolcd bootstrap` (see bootstrap.yaml); the self-signed certificate is not persisted (OPS-096).; patrol-server calls settings.EnsureServerSecretKey at startup and waits for the signing key to reach the settings snapshot. docs/verification/runs/2026-10-17-deploy-validation.md<br>_patrol-initial-admin-secret (key password) is created by patrolcd bootstrap with Create (exactly one concurrent run wins); the envtest test runs the real `patrol admin initial-password` command and gets the same password, which logs in as admin. Differences: 24 characters of [A-Za-z0-9] (upstream 16 of [0-9A-Za-z-]); the Secret is never updated in place: when the hash is missing an existing one is reused (crash recovery) unless it was created in an earlier second than patrol-secret, in which case it is deleted (UID precondition) and created again with a new password, as upstream generates a new one (re-install, DV-6; relies on API-server creationTimestamps, see bootstrap.md "Stale initial admin Secret"). Exercised by a kubelet-run install on kind (2026-10-19): the Secret created by the init container holds a password that logs in to the running server, and a re-install after a password change replaces it (the kind checks in tests). `patrol admin initial-password` itself ran only against envtest (the kind script reads the Secret with kubectl). Limits: chart default profile, one replica, kindnet, linux/arm64.; CLI part (admin initial-password) verified against envtest; patrol-initial-admin-secret is created at first start by `patrolcd bootstrap` (see bootstrap.yaml), which the install manifests and the Helm chart run as an init container.; The password written by the bootstrap init container to patrol-initial-admin-secret logs in to the emulated patrol-server pod (profiles with the admin account enabled, envtest) and, on kind (2026-10-19), to the real patrol-server pod of the chart's default profile, read from the Secret with kubectl and sent only through pipes. Limits: default profile only (the other admin-enabled profiles ran emulated), one replica, kindnet, linux/arm64. The `patrol admin initial-password` command was not run on kind (envtest only, bootstrap.yaml and cli-admin.yaml)._ |

Parity is argued from the algorithm and a differential test against an independent mirror of the baseline's structure; no upstream golden assignment exists to compare with. | [OPS-073](areas/10-operations.md#ops-073-argocd_app_orphaned_resources_count-gauge) | `argocd_app_orphaned_resources_count` gauge | GA | ---- | implemented | internal/controller/core/metrics.go<br>internal/controller/app/status.go<br>internal/controller/app/state.go<br>internal/controller/app/reconciler.go | internal/controller/core/metrics_test.go:TestOrphanedResourcesMetric<br>internal/controller/core/metrics_test.go:TestOrphanedResourcesMetricConcurrent<br>internal/controller/app/reconciler_test.go:TestReconcileForgetsOrphanedSeries<br>internal/controller/app/specerror_test.go:TestSpecErrorClearsTreeState<br>internal/controller/app/specerror_test.go:TestTreeRefreshOrphanedSeries<br>internal/controller/app/specerror_test.go:TestComparisonWithoutTreeKeepsOrphanedSeries<br>internal/controller/integration/orphans_test.go:TestOrphanedResourcesRefresh<br>_patrol_app_orphaned_resources_count{namespace,name,project}, set after every tree build (comparison or tree-only refresh): one series per Application, under its current project. Not emitted for Applications whose project does not monitor orphaned resources (upstream sets 0). Beyond upstream, a project change moves the series (upstream keeps the old project's series until the optional metrics cache expiration), and the series is removed when the Application is deleted, when its spec is invalid (no comparison; the stored tree is then empty, as upstream, so a tree-only refresh compares again), and when this replica stops processing it (namespace no longer allowed, cluster of another shard); an Application with skip-reconcile keeps it, as it keeps the rest of its state._ |

| [OPS-162](areas/10-operations.md#ops-162-bundled-tool-and-image-versions) | Bundled tool and image versions | GA | ---- | implemented | Dockerfile<br>hack/images/checksums.txt<br>hack/images/fetch-tools.sh<br>hack/images/apk-packages.txt<br>hack/images/rootfs-setup.sh | hack/images/verify-pins.sh<br>hack/images/rootfs-check.sh<br>hack/imagelayout/main_test.go<br>hack/images/smoke.sh<br>hack/kind/verify-install.sh:sync (Helm, Kustomize, git and OCI sources rendered by the running repo-server on kind)<br>docs/verification/runs/2026-10-19-image-build.md; docs/verification/runs/2026-10-19-kind-validation.md<br>_Single multi-call image on alpine:3.24.2 (index digest pinned), user patrol uid/gid 999, entrypoint tini; Helm 4.2.1, Kustomize 5.8.1, git-lfs 3.7.1 (baseline versions) downloaded from the official release URLs and checked against pinned SHA-256 sums (re-verified against the publishers' checksum files; Helm and git-lfs PGP signatures checked once by hand); git, openssh-client, gpg, tini, ca-certificates, tzdata from Alpine; connect-proxy compiled from a pinned source. Upstream ships Ubuntu 26.04 (glibc); Patrol uses Alpine (musl), see the guide. Built for linux/arm64 on 2026-10-19 (386 MB); `make image-layout-check` finds all 52 layout expectations in the image and `make image-smoke` asserts helm v4.2.1, kustomize v5.8.1 and git-lfs 3.7.1 (equal to the pins) by running the tools in the image. On kind (2026-10-19) the running repo-server of a rebuild with the same Dockerfile and pins (7c340fb-dirty, with the KV-1 fix) rendered the fixture Applications (Helm chart from git, Kustomize overlay, multi-source chart with $values, OCI Helm chart, OCI manifest bundle, plain manifests over HTTP and SSH), which hack/kind/verify-install.sh:sync asserts end Synced/Healthy/Succeeded. Missing for verified: the linux/amd64 image; `helm version` and `kustomize version` were run in containers of the 3ea2bd3 image, not inside the running repo-server pod._ |
